  unittest run excluding `test_lead_driven.py` completed with exit `0` at
  `19:23:11Z`, with `ResourceWarning` output in the pane. Ticket 21 context
  compacted at `19:23:45Z`; after resuming it performed a quick memory check
  against `/data02/home/philip.yang/.trae/cli/memories/MEMORY.md`, reread
  review guidance, checked status and scenario layout, and started a 17-scenario
  readiness loop at `19:24:56Z`. The pane showed the first five
  acceptance-focused scenarios passed and the loop still running. No new marker
  commit was observed.

### INFERRED

- The lead's status reply was broadly consistent with the monitor and direct
  git state: seventeen tickets landed, ticket 21 current with integration, and
  ticket 18 behind the integration tip despite having a ready-marker subject at
  its stale head.
- Ticket 18 moved from full harness validation into the broader tools sweep,
  but remained in dirty review-2 rework and still had no replacement handoff.
- Ticket 21 had a second context jump, this time from 26% in the 19:17 sample
  to about 90-91% after compaction, while still doing validation and scenario
  cleanup. The monitor again did not identify the jump as compaction.
- Both active workers were consuming long gate time after already having
  passing subsets. The visible bottleneck is not implementation churn in this
  interval but repeated broad validation before a clean final marker.

### DESIGN

- Public lead status should probably include the same behind/current facts the
  lead has to compute with direct git. The coordinator table's `ahead` column
  alone is not enough to explain why ticket 18 cannot land without a rebase.
- A first-level design should distinguish "status for owner" from "status for
  lead scheduling". The owner-facing status was accurate, but the scheduling
  details still required direct branch and pane inspection.
- Context compaction should be reported as an event with before/after context
  and current command, especially when it happens inside a long validation
  phase.
- Gate reports should distinguish pass-with-warning from clean pass. Both
  workers had green commands with repeated `ResourceWarning` noise that matters
  for process confidence even when the exit code is `0`.

### INFERRED

- The owner's new instruction changed the run from worker rework only into a
  cleanup/review/landing push, and the lead responded by adding ticket 22 plus
  a one-off disk-cleanup worker before reviewing the newly ready tickets.
- Ticket 18 and ticket 21 both reached clean ready markers shortly after the
  previous tick. Ticket 21 was current with integration, while ticket 18 still
  compared as behind the integration branch and therefore likely needed a
  rebase before landing.
- The cleanup worker solved an immediate host-resource problem but lived
  outside the normal ticket table, so the coordinator did not expose all active
  agents or all writes being performed under the effort.
- The no-host-`/tmp` decision became operational knowledge in several places at
  once: a record-branch decision, a new ticket, scratch coordinator code and a
  lead-side memory note.

### DESIGN

- First-level collaboration needs a first-class lane for lead-launched
  maintenance workers. They have the same coordination and safety implications
  as ticket workers, but the monitor currently hides them from the ticket view.
- Temp-directory policy should be part of the launch contract and preflight,
  not only a late note or memory rule. Ticket 22 exists because several earlier
  workers and the observer hit host `/tmp` failures.
- Ready notifications should be event-driven. Both ticket 18 and ticket 21
  became reviewable between periodic samples, and the lead had to discover that
  while also responding to a new owner instruction.
- Lead-side manual pane input is useful operationally but should leave a typed
  audit event. Otherwise later readers see only a prompt crossing in transcript
  metadata and a side effect in the pane.

### INFERRED

- Ticket 21 exposed a major handoff-quality gap: the worker's own final report
  said the suite passed, but the lead's clean-checkout gate reproduced a
  deterministic failure on the accepted Candidate.
- The lead recorded ticket 21 acceptance before the clean-checkout gate failed,
  then immediately recorded a rework request. The durable history now contains
  both an acceptance commit and a later "gate failed" review commit for the
  same Candidate.
- Ticket 18 was moved ahead of ticket 21 because ticket 21 failed its landing
  gate. The lead used a reintegration handoff rather than a substantive review,
  matching the stated queue-order change.
- Ticket 22 is broadening from tests into production launcher, worker,
  baseline and policy-doc changes while context is already low and active
  failures are still being debugged.

### DESIGN

- A Candidate should not be durably accepted until the lead-side landing gate
  succeeds, or the system should have a distinct "accepted but gate failed"
  state. The current sequence creates ambiguous durable records for ticket 21.
- Worker handoff should require a clean-checkout gate against the exact commit,
  not only tests in the worker's current worktree. Ticket 21 demonstrates that
  untracked, ignored, mode or environment differences can invalidate a green
  local report.
- Re-integration handoffs should be visibly distinct from review rounds in the
  monitor and artifact model. Ticket 18's message correctly said it was not a
  review round, but the state still appears as generic `rework`.
- Low-context workers making cross-cutting policy and launcher edits should be
  visible as a risk state. Ticket 22 was at 17% context with fourteen dirty
  files and ongoing failures, which is different from ordinary implementation
  progress.

  monitor's `0` ahead value is correct after the rebase start, but without the
  direct git status it does not show that the worker is mid-conflict.
- Ticket 21 made a focused fix quickly, then followed the lead's new
  clean-checkout instruction. Its branch was clean and one commit past the
  rejected marker, but the broad tools gate had not finished by the cutoff, so
  it was not ready for review again.
- Ticket 22's context jump from 17% to 77% indicates compaction during the
  interval; the monitor activity string did not explain that state transition
  beyond "Reflecting on setbacks".
- The same cross-cutting files are now active in both ticket 18 conflict
  resolution and ticket 22 implementation, especially `tools/agents/worker.py`,
  `tools/agents/lead_launch.py` and related tests.

### DESIGN

- Rebase conflicts should be a first-class worker state with unmerged path
  counts and conflict file names. A plain `running` row hides the highest-risk
  work in the interval.
- Clean-checkout gate progress should be separately visible from worktree-local
  validation. Ticket 21 passed the clean harness gate, then moved to a still
  running broad tools gate.
- Context compaction should be logged as an event with before/after percentages
  and current task. Ticket 22's jump upward happened while the worker was in a
  broad-failure recovery loop.
- Collision reporting needs to include unmerged files and dirty files together;
  ticket 18 and ticket 22 are now both changing core worker/launcher surfaces
  from different states.

  18 produced a marker-looking head, then immediately changed code and kept

  structured metadata; ticket 18's last three commits all read
  `ticket 18: ready for review`.

  remaining harness, broad tools and scenario sweep. Ticket 21's progress is

  "policy/tooling/core" scope or similar blast-radius cues, not just dirty path
  counts.

### INFERRED

- Ticket 21 and ticket 22 became clean, idle, ready handoffs during this
  interval, but no lead review or landing artifact existed by the observed
  cutoff. The lead had begun acceptance work, and its result was not yet
  visible in the transcript.
- Ticket 18's branch head continued to look like a ready marker, but the
  worker was actively modifying four files after that marker and cycling
  through failing then passing lead-launch tests. The monitor showed dirty
  count and a one-line activity, but not the failed-gate sequence.
- Ticket 22's final handoff hash changed three times within about one minute
  because the worker corrected the commit message after a managed hook added a
  duplicate trailer. A lead status sample taken during that minute saw
  `4450f81`, while the final ready hash was `738ead5`.
- The lead's 20:10 action bundled status triage, cleanup-worker window closure,
  report copying, ticket 21 report inspection and acceptance work into one shell
  command. That makes the start of lead review visible, but the individual
  substeps and any failure point are not durable until the command returns.

### DESIGN

- Ready-marker candidates need a stable lifecycle: tentative marker, amended
  marker and final handoff should be distinguishable, especially when hooks can
  rewrite or force amendments after a lead has sampled the branch.
- Monitor rows should show the last gate verdict and current gate suite for a
  post-marker dirty worker. Ticket 18 was not merely "running"; it had a stale
  marker, four post-marker dirty files, multiple failed lead-launch checks and
  one later passing focused suite.
- Lead-side review/accept/land commands should create typed in-progress events
  before long compound shell commands return. Otherwise observers and owners
  cannot tell whether a ready ticket is waiting, under review, or blocked in a
  lead-side gate.
- Maintenance-worker lifecycle events should be first-class. The disk-cleanup
  worker disappeared from tmux after a lead command, while the coordinator
  ticket monitor never represented that actor.

### INFERRED

- Ticket 21 landing used the newer record-branch flow, so the old cache state
  directory no longer shows the full landing story. The monitor and lead log
  agree that the ticket landed, but the artifact location has moved outside the
  original cache path the observer was told to watch.
- Ticket 18 lost its ahead commits from the monitor because the rebase reset
  the worktree to the new integration tip while applying its patches through a
  conflicted index. `0 ahead` plus `20 dirty` hides the fact that a ticket 18
  commit existed moments earlier and is now being replayed.
- Ticket 22 is stable and clean, but already stale behind the new integration
  tip. The monitor's ahead/dirty columns do not show the six commits it must
  rebase over before landing.
- The cleanup worker went from out-of-band actor to completed and removed from
  tmux, but its lifecycle is still mostly visible through lead notes rather
  than the coordinator monitor.

### DESIGN

- The monitor needs separate `ahead`, `behind`, and `rebase/conflict` signals.
  Ticket 18's `0 ahead` row was technically true but operationally misleading,
  and ticket 22's `1 ahead` row hid that it was six commits behind.
- Record-branch migration should update observer/monitor data sources or expose
  a unified read API. After ticket 21, the old state directory could not by
  itself explain why the monitor showed `landed`.
- A lead wait/hold reason should be durable for ready workers. Ticket 22 was
  content-accepted but intentionally held behind ticket 18's rebase, while the
  monitor still showed ordinary `ready`.
- Rebase replay of a worker's own just-created integration commit should be a
  named phase, with the lost/replayed commit hash retained for review.

### INFERRED

- Ticket 18 made real progress: the second rebase conflict was resolved and
  the worktree was clean at a new integration-aware implementation tip. It was
  still not a stable handoff because the tip subject was not a ready marker and
  the worker had moved into scenario-rule audit and follow-up edits after the

  the run was waiting primarily on ticket 18's rebase and verification.

### DESIGN

- A ready worker that subsequently commits a non-marker integration/adaptation
  commit should be shown as "verification pending" or similar, not merely
  `running`.
- The monitor should surface both the latest ready-marker hash and the current
  branch tip hash when they differ. Ticket 18 had marker commits in history but
  a non-marker tip.
- Rebase tooling should explain successful completion after interim
  `rebase --continue` failures, including which commits were replayed and
  whether a fresh marker is still required.
- Stale ready candidates should show behind count and "rebase held" reason in
  the ticket row; ticket 22 remained clean and accepted but blocked by ticket
  18's ordering.

  last failing scenario or test name, last verdict and rerun start time.

  marker context.

  the lead does not need transcript inspection to distinguish "still running"
  from "failed and rerunning".

  remained clean and idle, but the ticket row still did not show why it should
  not be landed immediately.

  changing `scripted_participant.py` changes what the readiness scenario is
  proving.
- Worker-initiated review commands, if allowed, need an explicit visible state
  and authorization boundary so they are not indistinguishable from ordinary
  worker self-checks.
- Monitor context should be interpreted with caution during long rework loops:
  ticket 18 dropped from 50% to 42% left over this pass without reaching the
  full-gate or marker phase.

  from "focused tests recovered; broad gate running" because those are different
  lead scheduling signals.
- Gate progress should include the last completed suite and the currently
  running suite. In this pass, the important state was "full worker and
  lead-launch green; full lead-driven still running".
- A ticket that is old enough to have a ready marker in history but now has
  dirty changes should display the last marker hash, current tip and pending
  marker requirement together.
- Long-running workers near 40% context should be highlighted when they are
  still before report and marker work, especially after multiple rework cycles.

- The monitor captured the clean `5`-ahead state and latest commit subject, but
  its activity text lagged the actual phase: it still said "Run narrow tests
  after conflict resolution..." while transcript and pane evidence showed a
  detached clean-gate run.

  clean detached gate running" separately from both dirty rework and ready
  handoff.
- Activity strings need to be tied to the current running command or command
  group; stale checklist text obscured that ticket 18 was no longer just
  running narrow tests.
- A clean non-marker tip should be visible as an intermediate state, especially
  when a worker is expected to create a final marker only after clean-checkout
  verification.
- Context warnings are more useful when combined with phase: 34% remaining
  before report and marker work matters more than 34% during a simple idle
  wait.

  run order.
- Ticket 18 stayed clean at the same non-marker commit but did not reach final
  handoff. The report timestamp stayed older than the current verification
  work, and both transcript and pane evidence still showed the ready-marker
  step pending.
- The 29% to 88% context jump for ticket 18, plus rereading guidance and
  starting a new detached gate path, looks like a compaction/resume boundary.
  The monitor exposed the new context value but not the compaction/resume or
  whether this was a repeated gate attempt.

  gate paths appeared in the same pass (`clean-gate.cYZlns` and

  earlier one in the monitor row.

- Worker monitors need explicit gate attempt ids, start times, current suite
  and final status. Otherwise a restarted clean gate looks the same as one long
  uninterrupted gate.
- Context compaction/resume should be visible as a typed event in the worker
  state, especially when it happens before the report and final marker.
- Landed worker panes should either be closed automatically or shown as stale
  monitoring debt; the lead had to manually close tickets 19 and 21 during an
  owner status check.
- Owner-facing status summaries are useful but should be backed by the same
  structured phase data as the monitor, so "running full gate" can be traced to
  the exact clean-gate attempt and suite.

- Source: transcript file stats at `2026-10-05T22:04:43Z`. The lead transcript
  remained `5704428` bytes. Worker 18 advanced to `43718874`; worker 19,

  `24555579` and `7539037`.

### INFERRED

- Ticket 18 was still in a legitimate wait on the restarted clean-gate attempt:
  it had completed the touched lead-launch area and all `tools/agents` tests,
  then spent the rest of the pass inside the slower lead-driven harness.
- The worker did not edit or commit during the observed interval; the ticket

  clean-gate command inside `bench/harness/test_lead_driven.py`.
- Ticket 22 remained a stable ready candidate waiting behind ticket 18, and
  integration did not move.

### DESIGN

- Clean-gate status should include "current command still alive, no new output"
  for long-running harness cases, so a quiet interval is distinguishable from a
  stalled worker or hidden prompt.
- Gate progress should be tied to the exact attempt path or id. This pass was
  still `clean-gate.ZDtV04`; that matters because the previous pass also had
  partial output from `clean-gate.cYZlns`.
- The monitor should show "report not refreshed" and "marker pending" beside a
  clean non-marker tip, because `0 dirty` alone can look deceptively close to
  ready.

  latest branch tip remained a non-marker implementation commit, the report had
  not yet been refreshed at the state-file sample, and the transcript showed
  report preparation rather than a final marker.
- The monitor showed only generic "Considering output approach" at the same
  time the worker had just completed a full clean gate and was entering report
  work. That is a different phase and a different lead scheduling signal.

- The lead's 22:08 answer was an owner-facing recap. It did not change

### DESIGN

- A clean-gate success should immediately become visible as a durable phase
  event: `gate passed; report/marker pending`. That is neither ordinary running
  nor ready.
- Report-writing after gate success is its own handoff phase and should be
  tracked explicitly; otherwise the lead sees a clean branch with no marker and
  must infer what remains from the pane.
- Worker evidence collection needs path helpers or canonical ticket/report
  paths. A wrong relative ticket path caused a small but visible detour after
  the expensive gate had already passed.

- The lead noticed the ready marker quickly and began review/probe work by

  result by the cutoff.
- The monitor accurately showed ticket 18 as ready, but it did not show that
  the lead had already entered a post-ready review/probe phase.
- Ticket 22 remained clean and ready, but still stale behind integration and
  intentionally waiting behind ticket 18.
- The worker's public note about an extra hook-added co-author trailer is a
  marker-hygiene concern to preserve for later process design; no observed
  lead action had addressed it in this slice.

### DESIGN

- `ready` should not be the final visible phase before landing. A first-level
  lead-driven monitor needs an explicit `lead reviewing` or `acceptance probe
  active` state once the lead starts post-ready validation.
- Probe attempts should have typed, durable in-progress records with path,
  start time, cleanup status and verdict, so observers and owners do not have
  to infer review progress from transcript snippets.
- Worker ready-marker creation should close the worker handoff phase but not
  imply that acceptance or landing has begun; those are lead-owned phases with
  separate evidence.

### DESIGN

- Landing should be represented as two separate transitions: integration tip
  moved, then ticket resolved/worker window closed. The gap is small here but
  visible and can affect dashboards.
- Record-branch acceptance/landing and legacy cache artifacts need one
  normalized read model. Otherwise "landed" is visible in monitor and record
  commits but absent as `landed.json`.
- Reintegration handoffs should be their own typed phase. This one was
  content-accepted but conflict-producing, which is different from a normal
  review rejection.
- Rebase conflict state should surface unmerged path names in the worker
  monitor as soon as git knows them, especially when the conflict is in shared
  coordinator code touched by prior tickets.

  to 81% during this pass, consistent with another compaction/resume event.

### DESIGN

- Rebase progress needs a finer-grained state ladder: `conflicted`, `conflicts
  resolved/index staged`, `rebase continued`, `focused checks passed`, and
  `clean detached gate running`.
- Monitor rows should include whether a worker is in detached rebase state and
  whether unmerged paths remain. `15 dirty` was much less useful once the `UU`
  paths had been cleared.
- Context percentages should carry a resume/compaction marker; otherwise a
  jump from low context to high context looks like recovered capacity rather
  than a session lifecycle event.
- A ready-marker commit produced by `rebase --continue` should be labelled as
  a rebased candidate awaiting final gate/report work, not a final handoff, if
  the worker's checklist still has gate and report steps open.

- The monitor's `finishing` state is closer than `running`, but it still hides

- A worker that has a clean rebased marker but is still running its required
  detached gate needs a state distinct from final `ready`. `finishing` is a
  useful start but should carry the active checklist item and command.
- Long gates should expose their current suite family and last completed suite
  in the monitor. Here the pane showed movement into
  `bench/harness/test_lead_driven.py`; the table did not.
- The protocol needs a clear rule for whether a rebase-produced
  `ticket N: ready for review` commit is a candidate immediately or only after
  the worker refreshes evidence and hands off again.
- Lead-owned run notes are now carrying retirement criteria for the interim
  coordinator; those criteria should move into a first-class effort state or
  decision before the next phase begins.

  second independent review, fix blockers or track findings, then mainline
  fast-forward. That sequence should live in a durable effort state rather than
  only a chat/status reply.

### INFERRED

- Ticket 22 advanced from "gate for rebased marker running" to "report-refreshed
  marker created and exact-marker gate running." It is closer to a stable
  handoff, but still not ready for lead review because the second gate has no
  final verdict.
- The monitor's `2 ahead, 0 dirty, ready for review` branch summary is
  technically accurate but misleading without gate-attempt identity. The first
  marker was no longer the candidate under final proof; the second marker was
  being proved.
- The lead made no new move in this interval. The work was entirely worker-side
  finalization after the lead's earlier reintegration instruction.
- Ticket 14 and ticket 15 continued to wait not only for ticket 22's code but
  for ticket 22's second exact-marker validation and lead landing.
- This pass shows another common first-level ambiguity: a worker can create a
  final-looking marker before the final gate that validates that marker has
  completed.

### DESIGN

- Candidate records should include a marker sequence or attempt id, so repeated
  `ticket N: ready for review` commits do not collapse into one indistinct
  monitor state.
- `finishing` should distinguish "report marker written; proving exact marker"
  from "pre-marker gate still running." The lead and observer both need that
  distinction to know whether the next action is wait, review or land.
- A worker-side final gate should publish current command id, exact commit and
  last completed suite in durable state. The pane exposed that detail; the
  monitor only showed a coarse running shell and context percentage.

  ticket as merely `ready` while the lead is actively landing it.
- Owner-facing status should distinguish "landing command started" from
  "landed", especially when the gate runs asynchronously and can still fail.

  `3ab9ec45cd46779632d19238f496903be9695287`. Ticket 22 remained
  `claimed`/`ready`, `2` ahead and `0` dirty, with PID `3942335`, disk `217M`,
  head `ticket 22: ready for review`, idle activity and 47% context left.
  Tickets 14 and 15 remained blocked and showed no worker.
- Source: direct git in the integration checkout and ticket 22 worktree at
  `2026-10-05T23:44:50Z`. Integration was still
  `3ab9ec45cd46779632d19238f496903be9695287`; ticket 22 was clean at
  `7d2f69cb274fe548940b1da4794a2ca2e243c43f`, comparing `0 2` against
  integration. The visible ticket 22 history was unchanged:
  `7d2f69c ticket 22: ready for review`, `244f3da ticket 22: ready for
  review`, then landed ticket 18 commit `3ab9ec4`.
- Source: ticket 22 state directory listing at `2026-10-05T23:44:50Z`. Ticket
  22 still had only `launch.json`, `reintegrate-1.md` and the earlier
  `reviews.jsonl`; no `landed.json` or new `review-*.md` file existed in the
  coordinator state directory.
- Source: transcript and scratch-file stats at `2026-10-05T23:44:50Z`. The
  lead transcript remained `5842413` bytes, unchanged from the previous cursor
  and still ending after the lead started background task `buqu2ydin`. The
  worker 22 transcript remained `10834955` bytes, unchanged from the worker's
  `23:30:59Z` handoff. `ws.py`, `lead-log.md` and `notes.md` were unchanged.
- Source: ticket 22 pane capture at `2026-10-05T23:44:50Z`. The pane still
  showed the completed exact-marker gate, clean final hygiene check and final
  handoff for commit `7d2f69c`, followed by an idle prompt with 47% context
  left.

### INFERRED

- Nothing observable changed after the lead started the background
  acceptance/landing task. Ticket 22 stayed ready and idle, and the integration
  branch did not move.
- The run is waiting on the lead-side background landing task or on the lead to
  observe its result. The coordinator monitor does not expose either condition;
  it still presents ticket 22 as simply `ready`.
- Ticket 14 and ticket 15 remained blocked even though ticket 22's worker-side
  handoff was complete, because ticket 22 had not landed.

### DESIGN

- Background landing tasks need visible lifecycle state and stale-duration
  reporting. Here the lead had said ticket 22 was landing, but ten minutes
  later the visible system still looked like a plain ready ticket.
- The first-level monitor should expose "ready, lead landing task running" or
  "ready, landing result not yet observed" so downstream waiters can tell the
  difference between worker delay and lead-side landing delay.

  traecli parity ticket 15.

### INFERRED

- Ticket 22's lead-side landing completed successfully between the 23:44 and
  23:54 passes, about fourteen minutes after the worker handoff and about
  fourteen minutes after the lead started the background landing task.
- The effort reached a major milestone: all build tickets, 01 through 13 and
  16 through 22, are landed on the integration branch. Ticket 14 and ticket 15
  remain later validation/trial work rather than build-ticket implementation.
- The old coordinator cache is now visibly incomplete for ticket 22 just as it
  was for several earlier record-branch landings: `ws.py status` and the
  integration branch know the ticket landed, but no `landed.json` exists in the
  old state directory.
- The lead moved immediately from landing the build tickets into whole-branch
  review and additional real-runtime proof work before launching ticket 14 or
  15.
- The monitor's ticket 14 wait list still includes ticket 22 after ticket 22
  landed. That appears to be a static dependency display rather than a live
  unresolved-blocker list.

### DESIGN

- The system should define a milestone state for "all build tickets landed;
  pre-mainline review/trial phase active", because the queue still contains
  blocked tickets whose purpose is different from ordinary implementation.
- Static dependency lists should be separated from unresolved blockers. After
  ticket 22 landed, ticket 14's row still named ticket 22, which obscures why
  it is actually blocked now.
- Record-branch landings need a single source of truth for landing artifacts;
  stale old-cache paths keep producing false negatives for observers and any
  tool still reading them.
- Independent reviewers should be first-class run actors with visible purpose,
  range and status, just like ticket workers, especially when their verdict is
  a gate before mainline.
